# Gova Repository-Wide Package Architecture Refactor Plan

## 0. Authority and Scope

This plan is the implementation blueprint for `not/package-architecture-refactor-goal.md`.

It is intentionally repository-wide. Existing package names, folder boundaries, public exports, composition roots, and dependency edges are evidence to inspect, not constraints to preserve.

The completed refactor MUST satisfy this invariant:

> **One Capability → One Owner → One Public Door → One Explicit Composition.**

This document is a plan only. It does not authorize partial compatibility architecture. The final implementation must be an atomic architectural cutover: no compatibility packages, forwarding exports, duplicate authorities, temporary dual ownership, deprecated package aliases, or old/new composition paths may remain in the completed tree.

Any newly created architectural unit MUST be a proper sealed Gova package that satisfies the repository package-creation contract. A new capability MUST NOT be introduced as an unregistered module, pseudo-package, arbitrary internal folder, parallel source tree, or other substitute for a project-compliant package.

---

## 1. Success Definition

The refactor is complete only when all of the following are simultaneously true:

1. Every existing package has been inspected by live source responsibility, not by name.
2. Every final package has exactly one coherent ownership statement.
3. Every capability, rule, service, adapter, repository family, runtime state machine, vendor SDK, and composition responsibility has exactly one owner.
4. No final package contains independent Domain, Application, Infrastructure, Data Access, Runtime, Transport, Platform, Presentation, Tooling, or Composition responsibilities that can change for different reasons.
5. No package imports application source through `@/`.
6. No composition package owns business logic or infrastructure logic.
7. Every runtime/deployment composes only the packages it actually needs.
8. The transitive package graph for every runtime is the smallest graph required by its reachable functionality.
9. Every capability-bearing or privileged vendor integration has exactly one authoritative adapter owner. Shared framework and pure utility libraries do not create capability ownership merely because multiple packages import them.
10. Every final package publishes one canonical public door: `.` only.
11. All removed package names have zero production imports and zero compatibility forwarding exports.
12. All existing application behavior, database behavior, mobile behavior, release behavior, service behavior, and deployment behavior remains functionally present.
13. Architecture enforcement makes regression into shared ownership or broad composition mechanically impossible.
14. The final architecture documents are generated from the new source of truth and match the live tree exactly.

---

## 2. Verified Current Baseline

The live repository currently contains **48 sealed packages**.

Current concentration indicators from the live tree:

| Package | Source files | Approx. LOC | Current public doors | Verified concentration |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | --- |
| `data-core` | 281 | 30,151 | 37 | DB drivers, schemas, sharding, repositories, domain entities, browser AsolDB, TanStack Query, image cache, provisioning, migrations, telemetry, runtime policy |
| `ota-core` | 85 | 9,746 | 6 | OTA domain, client runtime, React runtime, publishing, store adapters, R2, release/admin behavior |
| `architecture-core` | 47 | 8,793 | 1 | architecture enforcement; large but currently cohesive |
| `native-core` | 137 | 8,160 | 5 | many independent Capacitor/plugin capabilities under one owner |
| `release-core` | 32 | 6,213 | 5 | release pipeline, release console, build jobs, artifact analysis |
| `storage-image-manager-core` | 19 | 3,006 | 4 | UI, image upload lifecycle, processing, queueing, caching |
| `notifications-core` | 30 | 2,781 | 7 | notification domain, grants, delivery orchestration, provider adapters |
| `storage-core` | 50 | 2,721 | 3 | object-store transport, image rules, profiles, providers, orchestration |

Additional verified problems:

- The current generated package graph contains 68 production package-import edges.
- `data-core` is a dependency hub for unrelated business and infrastructure concerns.
- `data-core/src/domains` contains persistence/application code for auth, orders, products, profiles, notifications, advertisements, OTA, feature flags, follow, data health, backup, system logs, password recovery and more.
- `data-core/src/browser` combines AsolDB, TanStack Query, query persistence, image cache and reset logic.
- `auth-core` owns authentication/session behavior while `data-core/auth` owns user entities and persistence.
- `orders-core` owns order meaning while `data-core/marketplace-orders` contains substantial order application and persistence logic.
- `storage-image-manager-core` and `data-core/browser/image-cache` both participate in image caching.
- `account-bridge` mixes route ownership, service-origin routing, notification bridging, mobile credential storage and direct FCM delivery.
- `native-core` owns unrelated native capabilities and more than twenty plugin dependencies.
- `@aws-sdk/client-s3` is currently owned by both `storage-core` and `ota-core`.
- `google-auth-library` is currently owned by both `notifications-core` and `ota-core`.
- Isolated `*-composition` packages still import exact `@/features/*` implementation seams.
- `submain-composition` is particularly broad and therefore tightly coupled to application implementation structure.
- Some composition behavior is triggered by module-load registration rather than explicit composition execution.

Whole-repository audit scope verified on the same tree:

- 48 sealed packages and 45 registered application features;
- 7 isolated service deployments: control, notifications, orders, products, profiles, submain and sub2main;
- 189 files under `src/app`, including 123 App Router `route.*` handlers in the current filesystem scan;
- 46 code files under `src/core`, including a 17-file HTTP/API gateway area and a 21-file configuration area;
- 894 code files under `src/features` and 88 code files under `src/shared`;
- 175 code/tooling files under `scripts`;
- `src/core/api` currently mixes HTTP execution, API protocol, local-first read policy, telemetry and notification-grant response behavior;
- `src/core/config` currently mixes runtime resolution, public service origins, versions, database credentials, session secrets, notification credentials and operational configuration;
- `src/shared` contains canonical preferences, theme, locale, installation and session-runtime behavior that must not remain hidden ownership after cutover;
- current architecture documents disagree on historical counts (47/48 packages and 6/7 compositions), so live registries/source are the baseline and protected contracts must be migrated deliberately.

These are the starting conditions the plan must eliminate or explicitly classify.

---

## 3. Final Architectural Layer Model

Replace the broad current `capability` bucket with explicit package layers. Every final package MUST belong to exactly one layer.

| Layer | Sole purpose | Allowed dependency direction |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `domain` | entities, values, invariants, pure policy, validation | exact domain edges listed in the Final Package Contract Manifest only |
| `application` | use cases and orchestration through declared ports | exact domain/application contract edges in the manifest; never concrete adapters |
| `data-access` | repositories, persistence mapping, domain-specific SQL/query implementation | exact domain/application contracts + approved persistence infrastructure primitives |
| `infrastructure` | external SDK/driver/filesystem/network/storage adapters and low-level reusable I/O primitives | exact domain value contracts and infrastructure primitives only |
| `runtime` | stateful runtime engines, caches and lifecycle | exact domain/application/infrastructure/runtime edges; never configuration, data-access, platform, tooling or composition |
| `configuration` | typed environment/configuration decoding, runtime-role selection and capability-specific configuration policy | exact configuration-only edges; never business execution |
| `transport` | HTTP/API protocol, CORS, service routing, request/response mapping | exact domain/application/transport contract edges; configuration is injected by composition |
| `platform` | browser/native/device capability adapters | exact domain/platform edges; capability SDK ownership is unique |
| `presentation` | reusable UI/view model/presenter | exact domain/application/browser-safe runtime/presentation edges; never data-access/infrastructure/platform/tooling/composition |
| `tooling` | build, migration, release, provisioning, generation, repository tools | exact manifest edges; never part of product runtime unless that runtime explicitly executes tooling |
| `declarations` | immutable deployment/account metadata | declaration contracts only |
| `composition` | runtime/deployment wiring only | exact manifest doors required by that runtime; never another composition |
| `enforcement` | architecture registry, scanners, generated truth | no production package dependency |

### 3.1 Mandatory dependency rules

1. `domain` never imports application, data-access, infrastructure, runtime, transport, platform, presentation, tooling or composition.
2. `application` never constructs a database, vendor client, filesystem adapter, network adapter, native plugin or framework runtime.
3. `data-access` contains no business policy beyond persistence mapping and query semantics required by its owned repository contract.
4. `infrastructure` owns vendor integration, not business meaning.
5. `runtime` owns lifecycle/state, not canonical business entities or environment-key meaning.
6. `configuration` owns typed configuration interpretation only; it never performs business use cases, persistence, provider delivery or presentation.
7. `transport` maps protocol to application contracts and never performs persistence directly.
8. `presentation` never imports data-access, infrastructure, tooling or composition.
9. `tooling` MUST NOT enter browser, native application, service-request or production web runtime closures unless that runtime explicitly exists to run the tool.
10. `composition` contains wiring only and imports no application `@/` path.
11. No package imports another package through an undeclared subpath.
12. No package exports more than one public door.
13. The package graph MUST remain acyclic.
14. Layer legality is necessary but not sufficient: every direct `@asol/*` edge MUST also appear in that package's row in the Final Package Contract Manifest.
15. Presentation may import a browser-safe runtime only when that exact edge is listed in the manifest and no persistence/platform/infrastructure implementation crosses the presentation boundary.
16. Runtime-to-runtime edges are allowed only for explicit lifecycle sequencing in the manifest; they may never be used to create a replacement runtime hub.

---

## 4. Public Door Contract

The final package rule is stricter than the current repository:

```json
{
  "exports": {
    ".": "./src/index.ts"
  }
}
```

Every final package exposes only `.`.

Current patterns such as `./server`, `./browser`, `./ui`, `./tooling`, `./providers`, `./session`, `./super-admin`, and package-specific subpaths MUST disappear.

If code behind two current doors has different runtime requirements, infrastructure privileges, change reasons, or dependency closures, the code belongs in separate packages.

This converts the principle “one public door” from documentation into an enforceable package contract.

---

## 5. Registry and Architecture Enforcement Reconstruction

Before the completed cutover can be accepted, `@asol/architecture-core` must model the final architecture directly.

Each package registry row MUST record:

- `folder`
- `name`
- exact `owns` statement
- exactly one `layer`
- runtime targets
- exactly one public export door
- exact vendor modules owned
- whether I/O is allowed
- whether secret access is allowed
- allowed dependency classes and exceptional package edges, if any
- composition membership, if any

Add or strengthen architecture checks for:

1. one package folder ↔ one registry row;
2. one package ↔ one ownership statement;
3. duplicate/overlapping ownership detection;
4. exactly one public export door;
5. forbidden layer-to-layer edges;
6. package cycles;
7. package imports of `@/`;
8. composition-to-composition imports;
9. undeclared vendor imports;
10. duplicate privileged-vendor adapter ownership;
11. tooling entering product runtime closures;
12. package runtime-target violations;
13. composition closure larger than declared capability set;
14. missing composition dependency required by a reachable route/use case;
15. old package-name imports after migration;
16. old compatibility forwarding exports;
17. domain entity/type duplication across owners;
18. repository implementation located outside its registered data-access owner.

Introduce a canonical `COMPOSITION_REGISTRY` in architecture-core. For every runnable runtime/deployment, it MUST declare the exact allowed package set. The architecture check MUST compute the real transitive package closure and fail when the live closure has either an undeclared package or a missing required package.

---

## 6. Final Privileged Vendor Ownership Rule

**Objective:** Prevent a second infrastructure/platform authority from appearing through a direct SDK import while avoiding false ownership claims over ordinary shared libraries.

A vendor becomes uniquely owned when importing it grants infrastructure, platform, deployment, persistence, delivery, signing, storage, native-device, or other capability authority. Such a privileged/capability-bearing vendor integration MUST have exactly one authoritative adapter package.

Framework and pure utility libraries such as React, Next.js, Zod, formatting/UI helpers, and other non-privileged libraries MAY be imported by multiple packages when their layer rules permit it; shared use does not constitute logical ownership.

Mandatory privileged examples after cutover:

- `better-sqlite3` → `@asol/sqlite-core`
- `@libsql/client` → `@asol/turso-core`
- `drizzle-orm*` → `@asol/drizzle-core`
- `@tanstack/react-query*` → `@asol/query-cache-core`
- `@aws-sdk/client-s3` and `@aws-sdk/s3-request-presigner` → `@asol/storage-core`
- `google-auth-library` → `@asol/google-auth-core`
- `web-push` → `@asol/web-push-core`
- `maplibre-gl` → `@asol/map-renderer-core`
- `sharp` → `@asol/branding-assets-core`
- `heic-to` → `@asol/image-processing-core`
- `qrcode` → `@asol/qr-code-renderer-core`
- `nodemailer` → `@asol/mail-core`
- `fflate` → `@asol/archive-core`
- `@vercel/sandbox` → `@asol/vercel-core`
- every Capacitor/plugin SDK → exactly one corresponding native capability package

`supercluster` is a pure geospatial algorithm dependency, not a privileged SDK; it is nevertheless confined to `@asol/geo-core` because clustering semantics have one canonical implementation.

No “intentional dual ownership” exception remains in the final registry.

## 8. Target Package Architecture

Every package below is a target owner subject to the Package Boundary Admission Test in Section 25. Before implementation starts, the target set is frozen by the Final Package Contract Manifest: every row must have a proved admission reason, exact direct dependencies, runtime targets, privileged-vendor classification and composition membership. An unforeseen split or merge is not an implementation-time discretion; it requires an explicit amendment to this plan and the same admission evidence. Independent responsibilities MUST NOT be merged into a broader package than the approved target contract.

### 8.1 Architecture, contracts, declarations, transport

| Final package | Layer | Sole ownership |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `@asol/architecture-core` | enforcement | architecture registry, checks, generated architecture documentation |
| `@asol/api-contract-core` | transport | framework-neutral JSON transport naming/validation contracts only |
| `@asol/api-server-boundary-core` | transport | Web Request/Response adapters that apply API contracts at server/service boundaries only |
| `@asol/cors` | transport | CORS origin policy, headers and preflight only |
| `@asol/service-http-core` | transport | service route JSON/error/CORS response protocol only |
| `@asol/service-proxy-core` | transport | deployment-wide `/api/*` proxy/preflight CORS boundary only |
| `@asol/service-health-core` | transport | service liveness/configuration-presence report protocol only |
| `@asol/service-routing-core` | transport | Business API route/method ownership only |
| `@asol/http-transport-core` | infrastructure | the single low-level HTTP/fetch execution gateway |
| `@asol/api-client-core` | transport | ASOL API request/response protocol, URL construction and typed API error mapping through injected ports |
| `@asol/api-read-policy-core` | transport | route-level freshness/cache-policy classification and cache-key semantics only |
| `@asol/service-origin-config-core` | configuration | typed client-safe service-origin decoding for Business API owners only |
| `@asol/account-contract-core` | declarations | shared account declaration types and validation |
| `@asol/gova-account-declaration` | declarations | gova account metadata |
| `@asol/control-account-declaration` | declarations | control account metadata |
| `@asol/notifications-account-declaration` | declarations | notifications account metadata |
| `@asol/orders-account-declaration` | declarations | orders account metadata |
| `@asol/products-account-declaration` | declarations | products account metadata |
| `@asol/profiles-account-declaration` | declarations | profiles account metadata |
| `@asol/submain-account-declaration` | declarations | submain account metadata |
| `@asol/sub2main-account-declaration` | declarations | sub2main account metadata |

### 8.14 Canonical capabilities currently living primarily in `src/features`

The source audit must not stop at existing packages. The following registered application features contain verified canonical capability logic and therefore have mandatory target owners below. The host feature retains only Next/React/page/route-specific code identified in the final ledger; the ownership decisions in this table are not deferred to implementation.

| Current feature | Mandatory target ownership action |
| --- | --- |
| `app-reset` | create `@asol/app-reset-core` (`runtime`) for coordinated browser/device reset behavior through storage/notification ports |
| `cart` | create `@asol/cart-core` (`domain`) for cart/fulfillment/pricing rules, `@asol/cart-application-core` (`application`) for checkout orchestration, and `@asol/cart-runtime-core` (`runtime`) for the verified reusable persisted cart-store lifecycle |
| `contact` | create `@asol/contact-core` (`domain`) for contact-message/rate policy and `@asol/contact-application-core` (`application`) for the send use case; email delivery must use the shared `@asol/mail-core` infrastructure adapter rather than creating a contact-specific `nodemailer` owner |
| `onboarding` | create `@asol/onboarding-core` (`domain`) for onboarding state/schema/step rules and `@asol/onboarding-runtime-core` (`runtime`) for the verified persisted onboarding state lifecycle; keep page/form presentation in the host application |
| `network` | create `@asol/network-health-core` (`application`) for server reachability semantics; device network state stays owned by `native-network-core` |
| `qr-code` | create `@asol/qr-code-core` (`application`) for QR artifact rules and `@asol/qr-code-renderer-core` (`infrastructure`) as sole `qrcode` vendor owner; file saving goes through `native-files-core` |
| `sharing` | create `@asol/sharing-core` (`application`) for share/deep-link content and action rules; native execution goes through `native-share-core` |
| `specialty-chat` | create `@asol/specialty-chat-core` (`domain`) for chat capability/message/receipt/rate rules and `@asol/specialty-chat-application-core` (`application`) for conversation/request orchestration through identity/catalog/profile/product/notification ports |
| `profile-working-hours` | move canonical working-hours entities/rules into `profile-core`; move use-case behavior into `profile-application-core`; retain cards in application presentation |
| `vehicle-catalog` | move vehicle catalog entities into `catalog-core` and loading/cache behavior into `catalog-runtime-core`; do not create a second catalog owner |

Every other registered feature must also receive an explicit ledger decision. If its source contains only page-specific presentation, route adaptation, or host glue, it may remain under `src/features`. If it contains canonical domain/application/runtime/infrastructure ownership, that ownership must be extracted into a proper package before final acceptance.

## 9. Exhaustive `data-core` Decomposition Map

`data-core` MUST be deleted at the end of the migration. Its responsibilities are redistributed as follows.

| Current `data-core` area | Final owner |
| --- | --- |
| `core/database/*` driver-neutral contracts | `database-contract-core` |
| `core/database/schema.ts` mixed users/identity/OTA/feature/specialty schema declarations | split by semantic table owner into `identity-data-core`, `ota-data-core`, `feature-flags-data-core`, `specialty-chat-data-core` and any other table owner proven by the Responsibility Ledger; delete the mixed aggregator after consumers move |
| `core/database/product/product.schema.ts` | split table ownership into `product-data-core`, `product-review-data-core` and `pharmacy-profile-catalog-data-core`; no shared product-schema owner remains |
| `core/database/profile/profile.schema.ts` and `user-specialties.schema.ts` | split table ownership into `profile-data-core`, `profile-review-data-core`, `follow-data-core`, `seller-discount-data-core`, `system-log-data-core`, `release-state-data-core` and the exact profile subdomain owner proven per table |
| `core/database/notifications/notifications.schema.ts` | `notification-data-core` only |
| `core/database/advertisements/advertisements.schema.ts` | split into hero-slider, featured-marquee and trending-ribbon data owners by table |
| historical SQL migration files and Drizzle metadata | immutable migration artifacts executed by `database-migrations-core`; each migration/table is linked in the migration ledger to its semantic data owner, but historical files are not rewritten merely to match new package names |
| future migration generation inputs | semantic schema remains in the owning data-access package; `database-migrations-core` owns generation/application/verification tooling only and never becomes semantic schema owner |
| SQLite clients/cached connections/SQLite execution | `sqlite-core` |
| Turso/libSQL clients/retry | `turso-core` |
| Drizzle bindings/schema adapter helpers | `drizzle-core` |
| backend selection/data-source registry | `database-runtime-core` |
| `provisioning/*` | `database-provisioning-core` |
| `tooling/*` schema/database creation, provisioning and schema-sync commands | `database-provisioning-core` |
| `tooling/*` data migrations, shard migrations, migration application and DB verification commands | `database-migrations-core` |
| `browser/asol-db/*` | `asol-db-core` |
| direct generic Web Storage primitives extracted from host/runtime code | `web-storage-core` |
| direct generic CacheStorage primitives extracted from reset/runtime code | `web-cache-storage-core` |
| `browser/query/*` cache lifecycle/policies | `query-cache-core` |
| `browser/asol-db-persister.ts` Query persistence adapter | `query-cache-data-core` |
| `browser/image-cache/*` cache lifecycle | `image-cache-core` |
| `browser/image-cache/*` AsolDB persistence adapter | `image-cache-data-core` |
| `browser/workers/asol-push-sw.js` event/display/click behavior | `web-push-worker-core` composed by `web-push-worker-composition` |
| `browser/workers/asol-push-sw.js` direct notification-center storage writes | `notification-center-data-core` adapter wired into the worker composition; direct IndexedDB logic removed from worker code |
| `browser/workers/asol-push-sw.js` specialty-chat receipt semantics | `specialty-chat-core` pure receipt policy invoked through the worker composition |
| `domains/auth/*` | identity/authentication + `identity-data-core` |
| `domains/account-deletion/*` | account-lifecycle + identity/account data owners |
| `domains/password-recovery/*` | password-recovery + password-recovery-data |
| `domains/product/*` | product/product-review data owners |
| `domains/product-search/*` product mode | product-search/product-search-data |
| `domains/product-search/*` seller mode | seller-search/seller-search-data |
| `domains/profile/*` | profile/profile-review data owners |
| `domains/pharmacy-profile-catalog/*` | pharmacy-profile-catalog data owner |
| `domains/seller-discounts/*` | seller-discount data owner |
| `domains/follow/*` | follow data owner |
| `domains/marketplace-orders/*` | orders-application + orders-data |
| `domains/notifications/*` notification token/push-preference/recipient persistence | notification-data |
| `domains/notifications/*` specialty-chat preference operations | specialty-chat-data |
| `domains/advertisements/*` | hero/marquee/trending data owners |
| `domains/ota/*` | ota-data |
| `domains/feature-flags/*` | feature-flags application/data owners |
| `domains/data-health/*` | data-health application/data owners |
| `domains/dev-cloud-backup/*` | turso-backup + backup-core |
| `domains/system-logs/*` | system-log-data |
| `domains/super-admin/*` administrative user search/impersonation projection | `user-administration-data-core` |
| `browser/asol-db/*` generic IndexedDB engine/store primitives | `asol-db-core` |
| `browser/asol-db/*` current/guest/impersonation session helpers and auth lifecycle markers | `session-data-core` |
| cart store persistence | `cart-data-core` |
| onboarding draft persistence | `onboarding-data-core` |
| favorites persistence | `favorites-data-core` |
| page-save pending/journal persistence | `page-save-data-core` |
| page-snapshot persistence | `page-snapshot-data-core` |
| image-upload draft/blob persistence | `image-upload-data-core` |
| OTA local state/revocation/outcome persistence | `ota-local-data-core` |
| notification local stores | the five notification local data owners from Section 8.6 |
| observability monitor local persistence | `observability-data-core` |
| installation-state persistence | `installation-data-core` |
| theme/preferences local persistence | `theme-data-core` / `preferences-data-core` by semantic owner |
| `ports/telemetry.ts` | telemetry-contract-core |
| `ports/runtime-config.ts` | database-runtime-core |
| `ports/product-search-fields.ts` | product-search-core |
| `composition.ts` | delete after equivalent database/system-log wiring is represented only in the exact final composition packages |
| root `index.ts` / current subpath barrel exports | delete after every consumer moves to the final one-door owner; barrels never become a residual compatibility surface |
| current `tests/**` | move each characterization test with the final responsibility it proves; cross-owner tests become integration/composition tests and none are discarded without replacement evidence |
| `control-system-logs.ts` | system-log-data composed by control |
| `control-release-state.ts` | release-state-data |
| `control-ota.ts` / `ota-runtime.ts` | ota-data + composition |

Every table, index, migration, shard, browser store and persisted key receives one **semantic owner** in the Persistence Ownership Ledger. Operational migration tooling may carry immutable historical migration artifacts, but that does not transfer semantic ownership away from the domain-specific data package. A final scan must prove that no table/schema definition is authored by two packages and no migration is orphaned from the data owner whose state it changes.

No generic `data-core` replacement package may be created. Recreating a broad `data-platform-core` or equivalent would violate this plan.

The current Web Push worker is a separate execution context, not an exception to package ownership. Its source moves out of `data-core`, its direct IndexedDB implementation is deleted, and its public file becomes a generated external artifact owned by `web-push-worker-composition`.

The current development filesystem provider is removed from `storage-core`; `local-object-storage-core` owns filesystem I/O and consumes the canonical local-development layout while `storage-core` retains cloud object-store transport only.

---

## 10. Application Source Boundary After Refactor

`src/features/*` remains the application host layer only for framework-specific page/route composition, page-specific state and app-specific presentation that is not reusable architecture.

The following MUST move out of `src/features/*` when it constitutes canonical capability ownership:

- business entities and canonical validation;
- business use cases;
- repository contracts and implementations;
- shared application services used by service deployments;
- vendor adapters;
- reusable runtime engines;
- reusable React components already treated as sealed capability;
- service routing rules;
- environment capability definitions;
- cross-runtime port implementations that can be expressed entirely through package contracts.

After cutover:

1. Composition packages MUST NOT import `@/features/*`.
2. Service deployments MUST NOT rely on mirrored application implementation seams to satisfy package ports.
3. Route handlers/pages may import package doors and a runtime composition entry, but must not construct infrastructure themselves.
4. Any logic that isolated services need MUST live in a sealed package, not in `src/features`.

---

## 11. Composition Root Redesign

### 11.1 Final composition principle

Composition is defined by **runtime**, not by “register everything available”.

Each runnable target owns one explicit root with one synchronous/async entry function. That function wires only the package graph reachable by that target.

Final composition packages:

- `@asol/gova-development-composition`
- `@asol/gova-web-composition`
- `@asol/gova-static-composition`
- `@asol/gova-android-composition`
- `@asol/gova-ios-composition`
- `@asol/web-push-worker-composition`
- `@asol/control-composition`
- `@asol/notifications-composition`
- `@asol/orders-composition`
- `@asol/products-composition`
- `@asol/profiles-composition`
- `@asol/submain-composition`
- `@asol/sub2main-composition`

`gova-development-composition` is the only development application root. Do not add a second local-development composition alias.

Every one is a proper package and every one has exactly one public door.

### 11.2 Delete the broad main composition pattern

The completed architecture must remove the need for a catch-all `registerAppServerPorts()` that registers every server capability.

`src/instrumentation.ts` becomes a thin runtime selector that imports exactly one composition package for the current runtime role and calls its explicit entry function.

It must not contain capability wiring itself.

### 11.3 Browser composition

Replace the broad global browser registration with runtime-specific composition entry points.

For example:

```text
gova-web-composition
  → query-cache runtime
  → browser observability
  → page-save runtime
  → page-snapshot runtime
  → image cache
  → notification bridge when reachable

gova-android-composition
  → browser-safe capabilities required by Android
  → only Android-reachable native capability packages
  → OTA runtime only when enabled for Android

gova-ios-composition
  → browser-safe capabilities required by iOS
  → only iOS-reachable native capability packages
  → OTA runtime only when enabled for iOS
```

Do not register native, OTA, image, notification or observability capabilities solely because they exist in the repository.

### 11.4 Service compositions

Every service composition imports only final packages and its own account declaration package.

No `@/features/*` import is allowed.

A composition must expose an explicit immutable runtime object or explicit registration function whose fields correspond to the service's actual tasks. Missing capability means missing key, not a configured no-op.

### 11.5 Composition performance rules

For every composition:

1. static imports are preferred when the package is unconditionally required by that runtime;
2. dynamic import is allowed only for a genuinely conditional capability or to preserve a measured bundle/runtime boundary;
3. no dynamic import may be used as a substitute for correct package boundaries;
4. no module-load registration side effect;
5. no global “register all” barrel;
6. no import of a package that contributes zero reachable runtime operation;
7. startup validation is limited to the credentials/configuration of the composed capability set;
8. connections/clients are lazy until first use unless eager initialization has measured benefit;
9. composition closure size is measured and regression-gated.

### 11.6 Composition registry

For every composition record:

- runtime/deployment identity;
- direct package imports;
- allowed transitive capability set;
- vendor capability set;
- required secrets/config keys;
- prohibited capability set;
- expected route/task ownership;
- browser/server/native applicability.

A composition test MUST fail if an unused capability enters the closure or if a reachable task lacks its owner.

---

## 12. Service Deployment Ownership Reconstruction

Do not assume the current account split is architecturally optimal merely because it exists.

For each service account, build a table from live routes containing:

- method + route pattern;
- application use case;
- domain package;
- data-access package;
- infrastructure package;
- required secrets;
- required database/storage account;
- final deployment owner.

Then apply these rules:

1. a route belongs to the deployment that can satisfy the full use case with the least privilege;
2. do not grant a deployment an extra secret merely to preserve an existing route assignment;
3. do not split one use case across deployment compositions unless the split is an explicit network protocol with an owner package;
4. route ownership and capability ownership are different concepts and must be recorded separately;
5. if a current `submain`/`sub2main` composition is only a collection of unrelated tasks, split deployment responsibility only if the deployment model permits separate deployable accounts; otherwise keep one deployment composition but wire independent packages without creating a shared business owner.

The plan does **not** require creating more Vercel accounts merely to obtain SRP. Composition packages may legitimately wire many independent owners because composition itself owns no capability.

### 12.1 Mandatory Mobile Push unlock reassignment

**Objective:** Remove an existing least-privilege contradiction instead of preserving it during the package cutover.

`POST /api/notifications/mobile-push/unlock` MUST move from the current `notifications` route owner to the `submain` deployment. The live `notifications` declaration has notification DB/grant/provider credentials but neither the users database nor the mobile-push unlock key/blob, while the live unlock use case verifies user identity and consumes those mobile-push secrets. Granting those authorities to `notifications` merely to preserve the old route assignment is forbidden.

Final ownership is therefore:

- route/method ownership → `submain` through `service-routing-core`;
- authenticated unlock orchestration → `mobile-push-unlock-core`;
- user lookup implementation → `identity-data-core`;
- phone equivalence → `phone-core`;
- AES-256-GCM credential-envelope encryption/decryption → `mobile-push-crypto-core`;
- server unlock key and expected server blob → `mobile-push-server-config-core`;
- client-safe embedded blob → `mobile-push-client-config-core`;
- device credential lifecycle → `mobile-push-credential-core`;
- device-direct FCM transport → `fcm-device-core`;
- notification service deployment retains only server notification delivery/provider authority and receives no users DB or mobile-push unlock secret;
- `submain-account-declaration` marks `ASOL_MOBILE_PUSH_UNLOCK_KEY` and `ASOL_MOBILE_PUSH_CREDENTIAL_BLOB` as server runtime requirements for the reachable unlock route;
- `NEXT_PUBLIC_ASOL_MOBILE_PUSH_CREDENTIAL_BLOB` is owned only by client/static/native build configuration and is never treated as the unlock secret.

**Required evidence:** route inventory reports `POST /api/notifications/mobile-push/unlock` as `submain`; the notifications declaration contains neither user-database credentials nor mobile-push unlock secrets; the submain composition is the only service composition wiring server unlock; static/native closures contain only the client-safe blob configuration; secret-ownership scans show the unlock key in server-only configuration and never in Web/Static/Android/iOS client code.

---

## 13. Package Creation Contract for Every New Package

For each new package, implementation MUST perform all of the following in the same atomic refactor:

1. create `packages/<name>/package.json`;
2. expose only `.`;
3. add the package to the architecture registry with exact layer/ownership/runtime/vendor metadata;
4. add an independent package contract test;
5. add the root `test:<package>` gate or approved grouped gate;
6. update build/test/static-build gate chains as appropriate;
7. declare only actual npm dependencies;
8. prove no forbidden `@asol/*` dependency edge;
9. prove zero `@/` imports;
10. assign vendor ownership uniquely if applicable;
11. declare runtime targets;
12. add/update generated architecture docs through the generator, never by hand;
13. add composition membership only where runtime reachability proves it is required.

A folder that does not pass these requirements is not an acceptable substitute for a package.

---

## 14. Source Audit Method Before Moving Code

Before moving any source file, build a machine-readable **Responsibility Ledger** for every relevant production/tooling file across the repository: all 48 current packages, all 45 registered `src/features`, `src/core`, `src/shared`, `src/app` route boundaries, `scripts`, service source/mirror inputs, root runtime configuration files, and native Android/iOS bridge/build surfaces. Generated mirrors are traced back to their source owner rather than treated as independent owners.

Each ledger row MUST record:

- current path;
- current package/feature;
- responsibility category;
- canonical capability;
- final owner package;
- public/internal status;
- current consumers;
- final consumers;
- side effects;
- vendor imports;
- environment/secrets read;
- database/shard touched;
- runtime targets;
- whether the file is moved, split, rewritten or deleted.

No production source file may remain unclassified.

For mixed files, split by responsibility before assigning ownership. Do not move a mixed file intact into a new package and call the package single-responsibility.

---

## 15. Migration Sequence

The implementation must be executed as one architectural cutover in the working branch, but internally follow this dependency-safe order.

### Step 1 — Freeze and inventory

**Objective:** Establish a complete, reproducible pre-refactor truth set so no responsibility, route, runtime privilege, persisted contract, dependency edge, or behavior can disappear unnoticed during redistribution.

**Required evidence:** machine-readable baseline ledgers plus committed/archived before-metrics referenced by the verification report.

- record the 48-package baseline;
- generate the responsibility ledger;
- generate the current package graph, runtime closures, vendor-owner map, route-owner map and secret-access map;
- record all current exports/import consumers;
- record current test/build/smoke gates;
- record baseline service artifacts and runtime closure sizes for comparison.

### Step 2 — Upgrade architecture enforcement first

**Objective:** Make the target architecture mechanically enforceable before code movement, so every subsequent move is judged against the final ownership/layer/composition rules instead of relying on reviewer memory.

**Required evidence:** architecture tests that fail on deliberately injected dual ownership, forbidden layer edges, extra public doors, app imports and oversized composition closures.

- add final layer model;
- add single-door enforcement;
- add unique privileged-vendor adapter-owner enforcement;
- add runtime target and composition registry model;
- add duplicate-ownership and old-package-name checks;
- temporarily point checks at the declared target manifest used by the refactor branch so every migration operation is judged against the final architecture, not the old architecture.

This is enforcement scaffolding, not a compatibility layer. It must not create production aliases.

### Step 3 — Create leaf domain/contract packages

**Objective:** Establish stable inward-facing meaning and contracts before outward layers are moved, preventing repositories, transports and runtimes from becoming accidental owners of business semantics.

**Required evidence:** each domain/contract package is pure, single-layer, single-door, I/O-free and has zero dependency on outward layers.

Create/migrate pure packages first because all later packages depend inward on them:

- identity/authorization;
- catalog/product/profile/order/notification/image/storage-profile/OTA/logging/domain packages;
- telemetry/database contracts;
- account declaration contract;
- geo and other pure domains.

Remove duplicate canonical entities/types from old owners as each responsibility moves.

### Step 4 — Create infrastructure owners

**Objective:** Centralize every privileged external side effect behind one adapter authority so SDK credentials, provider behavior and platform access cannot leak into business/application packages.

**Required evidence:** privileged-vendor import scan shows exactly one adapter owner per capability-bearing SDK and zero direct privileged SDK imports from consumers.

Create unique owners for:

- SQLite;
- Turso/libSQL;
- Drizzle;
- browser Web Storage primitives;
- browser CacheStorage primitives;
- R2/S3;
- Google auth;
- Web Push;
- FCM;
- APNs;
- Vercel;
- App Store Connect;
- Google Play publishing;
- filesystem style store;
- MapLibre;
- each native plugin capability.

Move every privileged/capability-bearing vendor integration to its single adapter owner and fail the architecture check on any duplicate authority. Shared framework/pure-utility imports remain governed by the layer dependency contract rather than false ownership exclusivity.

### Step 5 — Create data-access owners

**Objective:** Give every persistence concern a domain-specific owner while removing the generic data hub and preventing SQL/schema knowledge from becoming shared infrastructure authority.

**Required evidence:** every repository/schema/query maps to one data-access package, generic repository registries expose no unrelated database, and persisted contracts remain behaviorally unchanged.

Move domain repositories and SQL/schema ownership out of `data-core` into their domain-specific data packages.

For each data package:

- depend on domain/application contracts;
- depend on DB infrastructure abstractions;
- own its schema/query mapping only;
- expose no generic datasource registry;
- expose no unrelated repository.

### Step 6 — Create application/use-case owners

**Objective:** Move reusable business orchestration out of framework/application folders and generic data packages so use cases depend on ports rather than concrete adapters or Next.js structure.

**Required evidence:** service/use-case code can be invoked from tests and isolated service compositions using package contracts only, with zero `@/` imports and zero direct infrastructure construction.

Move application services from `data-core` and `src/features` into application packages.

Application packages must receive persistence/storage/auth/provider behavior through constructor/factory arguments or explicit port registration owned by that package. They must not reach into application `@/` source.

### Step 7 — Split runtimes and presentation

**Objective:** Isolate stateful lifecycle engines from reusable presentation so browser/server/native graphs include only the runtime and UI code they actually execute.

**Required evidence:** runtime-target scans prove no server-only driver leaks into browser/static/native bundles, presentation packages import no data-access/infrastructure, and every runtime engine has one owner.

Move:

- AsolDB;
- browser Web Storage/CacheStorage adapters where runtime reset/draft behavior requires them;
- Query cache;
- image cache;
- image upload runtime;
- OTA runtime;
- observability runtimes;
- page-save runtime;
- system-log capture;
- reusable UI packages;
- native capability packages.

Ensure browser/server/native closures contain only compatible code.

### Step 8 — Rebuild composition packages

**Objective:** Turn composition into thin runtime-specific assembly with the smallest reachable graph, removing all dependency on application implementation seams and all hidden registration side effects.

**Required evidence:** each composition's declared package closure equals its measured live closure, every required task is present, every prohibited/unreachable capability is absent, and service smoke proves registration actually ran.

Rewrite every composition package from final package doors only.

Create gova web/static/native composition packages.

Remove all `@/features/*` composition seams and remove the architecture exception registry that permitted them once no consumer remains.

### Step 9 — Migrate application and service consumers

**Objective:** Make every consumer depend directly on the final owner/public door so no old path, application seam, mirror-specific import, or transitional alias remains reachable.

**Required evidence:** repository-wide import inventory reports zero old package names/subpaths, zero composition-to-application imports and zero service dependency on mirrored ownership code.

Rewrite all imports in:

- `src/app`;
- `src/core`;
- `src/features`;
- `src/shared`;
- `services/*`;
- scripts/tooling;
- Next/Capacitor configuration;
- test suites.

Consumers import only the final package owner.

No old package import may remain.

### Step 10 — Delete superseded packages and code

**Objective:** Enforce the hard cutover physically, not cosmetically, so old authorities cannot survive as fallback paths, aliases, compatibility facades or dead-but-importable packages.

**Required evidence:** removed-owner ledger is zero, filesystem/package registry contain no superseded package, and full-text/import scans find no compatibility forwarding path.

Delete old package folders only after their ownership ledger reaches zero remaining responsibilities.

Mandatory removals include at least:

- `data-core`;
- `native-core`;
- `account-bridge`;
- `account-declarations`;
- `auth-core`;
- `map-core`;
- `release-core`;
- `storage-image-manager-core`;
- `service-runtime-core`;
- old broad `observability-core`;
- old broad `vercel-deploy-core`;
- any other old package whose responsibility has been fully transferred.

Do not leave package stubs or forwarding barrels.

### Step 11 — Remove old composition infrastructure

**Objective:** Eliminate every architectural exception that existed only because the old package graph required application seams or broad registration, leaving one explicit runtime composition model.

**Required evidence:** composition seam exception registry is deleted or empty by design, broad roots are gone, module-load registration scan is clean, and instrumentation performs selection only.

Delete:

- application feature composition seam exceptions;
- obsolete broad server/browser composition roots;
- module-load registration patterns;
- compatibility route/wiring imports created only for the old package graph.

`src/instrumentation.ts` becomes runtime selection only.

### Step 12 — Regenerate architecture truth

**Objective:** Make documentation, runtime compatibility references, service manifests/locks and generated knowledge reflect the final live graph so the repository has one post-cutover source of truth and no stale architectural contract.

**Required evidence:** generated-doc drift checks, runtime-compatibility checks, service mirror/lock verification and docs CI all pass against the final package graph.

Regenerate package catalog, capability map, dependency map, feature/application documentation, runtime/deployment maps and any knowledge graph indexes whose source-of-truth inputs changed.

Update `config/runtime-compatibility-reference.json` through its canonical maintenance path so workspace package inventory, dependency fingerprints, service dependency snapshots and lock digests match the new graph.

Regenerate every service mirror/package manifest/lock affected by package or vendor relocation, then verify the mirror graph before build/smoke.

Update intentional architecture documentation in the same change. Because this refactor intentionally changes protected contracts such as package layers, package exports, package creation rules and composition rules, perform those edits only through the repository's explicit protected-contract authorization mechanism; generated architecture docs are regenerated, never hand-edited.

---

## 16. No-Dual-Ownership Procedure

For every capability transfer, use a three-state ledger during implementation:

```text
UNMIGRATED → MOVED → OLD_OWNER_DELETED
```

A capability is never marked complete while both old and new implementations remain reachable.

Rules:

1. move source or rewrite it in the new owner;
2. change all consumers in the same refactor branch;
3. delete old source immediately after its last consumer moves;
4. never add forwarding re-exports from old package to new package;
5. never duplicate repository/schema/service implementation during migration;
6. never publish both old and new package names;
7. never add compatibility aliases to package exports;
8. architecture checks must reject the old owner once the target ledger marks the capability MOVED.

---

## 17. Data and Schema Preservation Rules

This refactor changes code ownership, not persisted data contracts unless an independently justified schema change is required.

Therefore:

- do not rename database columns merely to match package names;
- do not rewrite historical migrations for architectural convenience;
- preserve existing Turso/SQLite schema semantics;
- move schema source ownership without changing the physical schema unless a separate migration is explicitly required;
- preserve current camelCase application/transport contracts and snake_case SQL/database row boundaries;
- preserve shard routing and account credential isolation;
- prove data-access packages produce the same externally observable data contract before and after the move.

---

## 18. Composition Performance Verification

“Better composition performance” must be measured, not assumed.

Capture before/after metrics for every runtime/deployment:

- direct package count;
- transitive package count;
- vendor module count;
- server trace/file count;
- startup-import module count;
- composition initialization duration in a controlled local measurement;
- server artifact size;
- static/mobile bundle contribution where measurable;
- number of port registrations;
- number of dynamic imports in composition code;
- number of credentials/env keys required at startup.

Acceptance rules:

1. no runtime may gain an unrelated capability in its transitive closure;
2. no isolated service may gain a vendor SDK it does not execute;
3. no gova frontend runtime may regain DB/storage/business-service packages;
4. no static/mobile runtime may include server-only DB drivers;
5. composition initialization must execute only required registrations;
6. dynamic import count must decrease or each remaining dynamic import must have a documented runtime-boundary reason;
7. broad root import cost must be eliminated by runtime-specific roots.

Performance must never be improved by weakening ownership, validation, security or least privilege.

---

## 19. Required Test Strategy

### 19.1 Package contract tests

Every package must test:

- only `.` is exported;
- no forbidden app import;
- no forbidden layer import;
- vendor ownership compliance;
- runtime target compliance;
- ownership-specific invariants.

### 19.2 Architecture tests

`architecture:check` must prove:

- package inventory exactness;
- layer DAG validity;
- no cycles;
- no dual privileged-vendor adapter ownership;
- no dual capability ownership;
- no old package references;
- exact composition closures;
- no `@/` imports from packages;
- no composition imports from application source;
- no undeclared package door;
- no tooling leakage into product runtimes.

### 19.3 Behavior characterization

Before moving each major capability, preserve/extend characterization tests for its observable behavior. Run the same behavior tests against the final owner.

Mandatory families include:

- authentication/session/account deletion/recovery;
- product/profile/search/reviews;
- orders and delivery flows;
- notifications/grants/providers;
- storage/image upload/cache;
- OTA update/admin/publishing;
- data health/backup;
- system logs/observability;
- release/deployment tooling;
- native capability adapters;
- catalog and advertisements.

### 19.4 Runtime and deployment tests

Verify each final composition independently:

- gova local development;
- gova web production;
- gova static export;
- Android static/native shell;
- iOS static/native shell;
- control;
- notifications;
- orders;
- products;
- profiles;
- submain;
- sub2main.

Each composition test must assert both positive capability presence and negative capability absence.

### 19.5 Full repository gates

The final cutover must pass all repository-required gates, including at minimum the applicable forms of:

```text
architecture:check
docs:ci
typecheck
lint
unit/package tests
service composition tests
services:sync
services:verify
services:build
smoke:services
main application build
static build
mobile/native preflight gates
release-related contract tests
```

Use the repository's current canonical command names at implementation time rather than assuming this list remains unchanged.

### 19.6 Observable-contract parity gates

The cutover MUST compare pre-refactor and final behavior, not only final success:

- **HTTP parity:** snapshot every one of the 211 current method+path rows with owner, auth requirement, request/response schema, status/error mapping, cache/CORS policy and side effects. Final inventory must contain the same product HTTP contract unless this plan explicitly reassigns deployment ownership; the known Mobile Push unlock move changes deployment owner only, not method/path/payload behavior.
- **Persistence parity:** snapshot SQL schemas/indexes/migration journals/shard routing plus all AsolDB store names/versions/key families. Final owners must reproduce the same physical contracts or carry an explicit tested migration; package movement alone is never permission to change data.
- **Configuration parity:** snapshot environment-key names, visibility class (client/server/tooling), required/optional semantics and consuming deployment. A key may move to a narrower owner, but no required runtime value may disappear and no secret may become visible to a broader runtime.
- **Native parity:** map every current TypeScript native API plus every Android Java/iOS Swift plugin/bridge/permission/resource to a final package or registered external artifact, with the same observable capability and failure semantics.
- **Public-artifact parity:** compare worker/manifest/catalog/branding/static generated artifacts by semantic contract and generator ownership, not merely filename.
- **Operational parity:** characterize Control build jobs, artifact analysis, Google Play operations, remote release status/callback, backup/data-health/system-log operations before movement and rerun the same scenarios through final owners.

Any baseline row with no final counterpart, migration, or explicitly approved removal is a **hard failure**.

### 19.7 Non-destructive build/runtime verification

Because `build:static` writes the release `out/` directory, final static/native verification MUST run from a disposable verification worktree or other isolated output copy at the final revision. The canonical working tree's release artifact must not be overwritten merely to prove the refactor. In that isolated verification environment:

1. run the normal Web build (`npm run build`);
2. run the canonical static build and verify remote API behavior;
3. run Android runtime checks, native preflight and an unsigned/debug compile/build path that cannot publish;
4. run iOS runtime/push/SPM policy checks and a code-signing-disabled simulator/debug compile on the authorized macOS build machine;
5. never invoke store upload, TestFlight, production Play, production Vercel deploy or OTA publish as a verification side effect.

A compile/build failure on any required surface blocks architectural completion even if unit tests pass.

---

## 20. Acceptance Audit for Every Final Package

Before declaring any package complete, answer YES to all of these:

1. Can its ownership be described without “and” joining independent concerns?
2. Does every production file serve that ownership statement?
3. Is there exactly one public door?
4. Does it have exactly one architectural layer?
5. Does it avoid `@/` imports?
6. Are all privileged/capability-bearing vendor integrations uniquely owned, while shared framework/utility imports are classified explicitly?
7. Are all I/O side effects appropriate for its layer?
8. Are all dependencies necessary for its responsibility?
9. Does removing any dependency remove an actual required behavior?
10. Is the package absent from runtimes that do not use it?
11. Is the same capability absent from every other package?
12. Does its test gate verify the ownership boundary, not merely function output?

Any NO blocks completion.

---

## 21. Final Repository Acceptance Gates

The architecture is accepted only when all of these repository-wide assertions hold:

### Ownership

- zero dual capability owners;
- zero duplicate repository implementations;
- zero duplicate canonical domain entities;
- zero duplicate privileged-vendor adapter owners;
- zero unregistered package folders;
- zero capability-owning modules outside packages.

### Dependency graph

- zero package cycles;
- zero package `@/` imports;
- zero composition-to-composition imports;
- zero forbidden layer edges;
- zero undeclared public subpaths;
- zero old removed-package imports.

### Composition

- every runtime has one explicit root;
- every root has a declared exact closure;
- no catch-all application root remains;
- no composition imports application implementation paths;
- no hidden top-level registration remains;
- no runtime loads a capability it cannot reach.

### Runtime safety

- browser/static/native graphs contain no server DB drivers;
- isolated service graphs contain only their credentials/capabilities;
- gova frontend remains free of business DB/storage owners;
- all required isolated-service backend pins remain enforced where a runtime cannot support both backends.

### Feature preservation

- all characterization tests pass against the final owner;
- the 211-row HTTP baseline has zero missing method/path contract and only explicitly planned deployment-owner changes;
- SQL/Turso/SQLite schema, migration-history, shard-routing and browser-local persistence parity has zero unexplained drift;
- every current mobile/native TypeScript/Java/Swift capability has a final owner and passes runtime/native verification;
- all public/generated artifacts have one generator/source owner and preserve their consumer contract;
- all Control operational use cases (build jobs, artifact analysis, Google Play, remote release, data health, backup, system logs) remain executable through their final package owners;
- all service routes remain owned and reachable;
- Web build, isolated Static build, Android non-publishing build checks and iOS non-publishing compile checks pass;
- all release/deploy capabilities remain available from their new owners;
- all persisted data remains readable/writable under unchanged external semantics.

### Documentation

- generated architecture docs match the registry/live graph;
- intentional docs describe the new layer model and composition model;
- old counts, old package names and old composition guidance are removed;
- an ADR records the repository-wide ownership reset and its invariants.

---

## 22. Completion Artifacts

The implementation is not complete until it produces all of the following final artifacts:

1. final package inventory;
2. final ownership registry;
3. final package dependency DAG;
4. final vendor classification/owner map (privileged adapter owners plus explicitly shared non-privileged libraries);
5. final runtime/composition closure map;
6. final route → use case → capability → data/infrastructure → deployment ownership map;
7. final old-path → new-package migration ledger with zero unresolved rows;
8. final removed-package list;
9. final architecture ADR;
10. regenerated capability map/package catalog/dependency map;
11. before/after composition performance report;
12. verification report containing all required gate results.

## 23. Explicit Prohibitions

The implementation MUST NOT:

- create a replacement mega-package for `data-core`;
- create a replacement mega-package for `native-core`;
- use subpath exports to simulate packages;
- create internal modules as substitutes for required packages;
- preserve old imports through re-export aliases;
- allow two packages to own the same privileged/capability-bearing SDK adapter authority;
- treat ordinary shared framework/utility imports as ownership merely because they are reused;
- allow two packages to own the same business capability;
- move code without re-evaluating its responsibility;
- put repositories in domain packages;
- put business rules in data-access packages;
- put vendor clients in application packages;
- make composition packages depend on `src/features`;
- make runtime composition depend on tooling packages;
- load every capability at application startup;
- optimize bundle/startup cost by silently weakening validation or least privilege;
- leave documentation describing the previous ownership model after cutover.

---

## 24. Final Definition of Done

The repository-wide refactor is DONE only when:

```text
Every capability has exactly one package owner.
Every package has exactly one responsibility.
Every package has exactly one public door.
Every privileged/capability-bearing vendor integration has exactly one authoritative package owner.
Every runtime has exactly one explicit composition root.
Every composition imports only packages, never application implementation seams.
Every runtime closure is minimal and verified.
Every old superseded owner is physically deleted.
Every feature remains behaviorally present.
Every architecture, build, service, static and mobile gate passes.
```

At that point the repository has moved from broad capability containers and centralized wiring toward a sealed, single-owner, runtime-specific architecture whose dependency graph and composition behavior are mechanically enforced rather than conventionally documented.

---

## 25. Package Boundary Admission Test

**Objective:** Achieve package-level SRP without replacing today's mega-packages with hundreds of microscopic packages whose coordination cost, build cost and dependency graph are worse than the problem being solved.

A proposed package is admitted only when its responsibility has at least one independent architectural reason to exist:

1. it owns distinct canonical domain meaning;
2. it owns an independently testable application use case family;
3. it owns a persistence/schema/query authority that must be isolated from other domains;
4. it owns a privileged vendor/platform adapter;
5. it owns a stateful runtime lifecycle with a different runtime closure;
6. it owns a transport/protocol boundary;
7. it owns a reusable presentation capability with an independent dependency/runtime boundary;
8. it owns tooling that must never enter production runtime graphs;
9. it owns configuration/secrets with a distinct least-privilege boundary;
10. it is a deployment/runtime composition root.

A new package MUST NOT be admitted solely because a file is large, because a directory exists, or because a helper could technically be reused.

Before creating any proposed package, record: change reason, layer, runtime targets, consumers, ports, privileged vendors, secrets, persistence, expected fan-in/fan-out and why merging it with the nearest owner would violate SRP, least privilege, runtime isolation or dependency direction.

**Required evidence:** a Target Package Admission Ledger with one PASS row per final package and zero `UNDECIDED` rows.
---

## 26. Exhaustive Application Feature Disposition Matrix

**Objective:** Prevent canonical ownership from escaping the package refactor by remaining hidden inside `src/features`. Every one of the 45 registered application features has an explicit post-cutover role.

`Host-only` means the feature may retain Next.js/React/page-specific presentation or route adaptation only. It may not retain canonical domain policy, reusable use cases, persistence, privileged vendor adapters, reusable runtime engines or composition authority.

| Current feature | Final canonical owner(s) | Allowed host remainder | Objective |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `account-bridge` | `service-routing-core`, `service-origin-config-core`, `notification-bridge-core` | delete feature or retain zero-logic host adapter only | remove cross-account routing/bridge authority from application source |
| `advertisements` | hero-slider, featured-marquee and trending-ribbon domain/application/data packages | page/admin presentation and route adapters | keep three advertisement capabilities independently owned |
| `app-reset` | `app-reset-core` plus storage/notification runtime ports | settings/UI trigger only | centralize destructive reset sequencing without owning underlying stores |
| `auth` | phone, identity, authentication, session, authorization and account-lifecycle package families | login/registration/account UI and route adapters | remove canonical auth/security logic from Next feature code and detach reusable phone semantics from auth ownership |
| `cart` | `cart-core`, `cart-application-core`, `cart-runtime-core` | cart page/components only | separate cart rules, orchestration and state lifecycle |
| `catalog-studio` | `catalog-studio-core`, `catalog-studio-application-core`, `catalog-studio-tooling-core` | developer console presentation/routes | isolate filesystem transaction tooling from catalog domain and UI |
| `categories` | `catalog-core`, `catalog-runtime-core` | category/seller browsing presentation | make canonical catalog loading/query semantics package-owned |
| `contact` | `contact-core`, `contact-application-core`, `mail-core` | contact form/page and HTTP adapter | separate contact policy/use case from generic mail transport |
| `data` | final database/config/composition package contracts | none; remove feature when ports are package-owned | eliminate the application-level `data-core` wiring facade |
| `data-health` | data-health domain/application/data packages | developer inspection/cleanup presentation/routes | keep health policy/use cases/data access independently owned |
| `dev-cloud-backup` | `backup-core`, `turso-backup-core` | developer console presentation/routes | keep backup orchestration independent from database adapter |
| `dev-tools` | `development-context-core` / `local-development-layout-core` plus the specific tooling owner | developer-only presentation | prevent a generic developer feature from becoming capability owner |
| `favorites` | `favorites-core`, `favorites-application-core`, `favorites-ui-core` | host slot wiring only | separate favorite meaning, operations and presentation |
| `feature-flags` | feature-flag domain/application/data packages | route adapter and any page-specific UI | remove flag persistence/evaluation authority from feature code |

| `google-play-console` | `google-play-store-assets-core`, `google-play-operations-core`, `google-play-assets-data-core`, `google-play-publishing-core`, `google-play-config-core`, `build-job-core`, `build-job-application-core` | super-admin console presentation/routes only | preserve every live Google Play read/edit/image/track/promotion/Fastlane entry while separating pure store rules, API transport, backup persistence and build-command execution |

| `navigation` | native app/browser primitives where required | application-shell navigation presentation | keep routing/navigation chrome host-specific rather than inventing a domain |
| `network` | `network-health-core`, `network-health-runtime-core`, `network-health-ui-core`, `native-network-core` | root-layout slot only | separate reachability semantics, lifecycle/state, presentation and native device connectivity |
| `notifications` | notification domain/grants/delivery/data/provider/native packages | notification-center/settings presentation and thin route adapters | dismantle the current feature mega-module into single-owner capabilities |

**Required evidence:** the final `APPLICATION_FEATURES` registry and responsibility ledger contain all 45 rows above, and a source scan proves every feature classified as host-only contains only presentation, framework routing/adaptation or local composition glue permitted by the final application-host contract.
