## 27. Non-Package Ownership Surface Matrix

**Objective:** Ensure the refactor covers the whole repository rather than making `packages/` clean while equivalent ownership remains hidden in application, script, configuration, service or native-shell folders.

| Current surface | Final treatment | Objective |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `src/app/**` | page/layout/route protocol adapters only; every route maps to package-owned use cases | keep Next App Router as framework host, never capability owner |
| `src/core/api/**` | dissolve into `http-transport-core`, `api-client-core`, `api-read-policy-core`, observability/cache/notification ports | separate HTTP I/O, protocol, freshness policy and response side effects |
| `src/core/config/**` | dissolve canonical config into `runtime-context-core` and capability-specific configuration packages | remove the current global config/secrets knowledge hub |
| `src/core/composition/**` | replace with package composition roots plus a minimal runtime selector | remove central register-all and application seam wiring |
| `src/core/providers/**` | retain only application React host composition or move reusable providers to presentation packages | prevent provider trees from hiding runtime ownership |
| `src/features/**` | governed by Section 26 | prevent canonical capabilities remaining in feature folders |
| `src/shared/i18n/**` | locale domain/runtime/presentation packages | give locale/translation behavior explicit owners |
| `src/shared/preferences/**` | preference domain/data/presentation packages | separate preference meaning, persistence and React state |
| `src/shared/theme/**` | theme domain/data/runtime/presentation packages; CSS assets remain owner-declared artifacts | separate theme policy, storage, document lifecycle and UI |
| `src/shared/installation/**` | installation domain/runtime packages | isolate installation policy from application boot component |
| `src/shared/session-runtime/**` | `session-ui-core` | keep React session context separate from session authentication authority |
| `src/shared/ui/**` and `src/shared/layouts/**` | host-only generic presentation unless a boundary admission test proves a reusable package | avoid both hidden business ownership and needless UI micro-packages |
| `src/shared/phone/**` | presentation model only; canonical phone parsing/validation/country rules live with `phone-core` | prevent a second phone-domain implementation and avoid forcing UI consumers through auth ownership |
| `src/shared/app-init/**` | `app-bootstrap-core` consuming theme/locale contracts | remove duplicated pre-module domain logic and centralize first-paint sequencing |
| `src/shared/dom/**` | generated/runtime artifact registered to its enforcement or presentation owner | prevent generated metadata becoming an unowned authority |
| `scripts/**` | thin CLI/orchestration entrypoints; reusable algorithms move to tooling packages | stop scripts from being an unsealed second implementation layer |
| `services/*/**` | deployment host plus generated/projected source only; never an independent capability owner | keep service mirrors as deployment artifacts, not forks |
| `next.config.ts` | framework configuration consuming final package/config contracts only | prevent Next config from becoming a capability/vendor owner |
| `capacitor.config.ts` | native shell configuration consuming `native-build-core`/branding contracts only | keep plugin policy in native package ownership |
| `package.json` scripts | invoke canonical package/tool entrypoints; no superseded package-internal paths | make command surface follow final ownership |
| `config/runtime-compatibility-reference.json` | regenerated canonical compatibility snapshot of final workspaces/services/vendors | keep compatibility policy synchronized with ownership changes |
| `android/**` | native host/project artifacts plus explicitly mapped platform-owned native code | prevent native project files from silently duplicating JS capability ownership |
| `ios/**` | native host/project artifacts plus explicitly mapped platform-owned native code | same single-owner guarantee for iOS |
| `public/**` canonical data/assets | remain physical public artifacts only when an `EXTERNAL_ARTIFACT_OWNERS` registry maps each canonical family to one package owner; `public/asol-push-sw.js` is generated solely from `web-push-worker-composition`/`web-push-worker-core` sources | give out-of-package catalog/branding/static data an explicit owner without forcing runtime bytes into package source |
| migrations/schema files outside owners | relocate or explicitly register to the owning data-access/tooling package | ensure schema authority is never path-based or ownerless |

Introduce an `EXTERNAL_ARTIFACT_OWNERS` registry for unavoidable repository-root/native/public artifacts. Each row records path/pattern, canonical owner package, mutability, generator (if any), and runtime consumers. An external artifact never becomes an independent owner.

**Required evidence:** every production/tooling path is either inside a registered package, a permitted host-only surface, generated output traced to a source owner, or an explicitly registered external artifact. Zero `UNOWNED` paths remain.
---

## 28. Route, Service and Secret Ownership Ledger

**Objective:** Prove that every HTTP surface is backed by exactly the packages, credentials and deployment authority required by its complete use case, with no route assignment preserved merely for historical convenience.

Current deployment scan to reconcile during Step 1:

| Deployment | Current route files in service tree |
| --- | ---: |
| control | 54 |
| notifications | 2 |
| orders | 2 |
| products | 4 |
| profiles | 9 |
| sub2main | 19 |
| submain | 39 |

The live canonical application scan currently contains 123 App Router `route.*` handlers. `npm run api:inventory` currently resolves those handlers to **211 method+path ownership rows across 121 unique API paths**: 57 `control`, 63 `gova/dev` (primarily local/CORS/OPTIONS behavior), 5 `notifications`, 1 `orders`, 3 `products`, 8 `profiles`, 27 `sub2main`, and 47 `submain`. The implementation MUST regenerate this inventory at cutover time and reconcile it with generated/context tooling; no route may be omitted because of filename-extension assumptions or stale historical counts.

The final route ledger starts from these 211 live method rows, not from route-file counts alone. The explicit exception already decided in Section 12.1 is `POST /api/notifications/mobile-push/unlock`: its **current** owner is `notifications`, while its **final** owner is `submain`. `OPTIONS` remains a host/CORS boundary and does not confer business ownership. Any additional reassignment discovered by the method-level least-privilege audit must be recorded with the same evidence before implementation.

For every method+path, record: source route, current deployment, final deployment, transport contract, application use case, domain owner, data-access owner, infrastructure/platform owners, configuration owner, exact secrets/env keys, database/storage account, notification side effects, CORS policy and composition root.

**Required evidence:** zero unmapped routes, zero route with two deployment owners for the same method, zero undeclared secret edge, route-ownership coverage tests, service mirror parity and positive deployed/local service smoke for each composition.
---

## 29. Compatibility-Layer Eradication Audit

**Objective:** Make the goal's “no compatibility layers remain” rule testable across package paths, source aliases, deprecated symbols, runtime fallbacks and migration shims rather than limiting it to removed package folders.

Before cutover, inventory every production occurrence of terms/patterns such as `deprecated`, `legacy`, `compatibility`, `shim`, forwarding re-export, fallback alias, old env-key alias, old route alias and old persistence-format fallback.

For every row, classify it as exactly one of:

1. **internal architecture compatibility** → MUST be removed in the final tree;
2. **historical persisted-data migration** → migrate data/state to the canonical representation during the cutover and remove the compatibility read/write path;
3. **external protocol/product interoperability** → may remain only when it is a current product requirement, has one canonical owner, is not an old package/API ownership shim, and is explicitly documented as product behavior rather than refactor compatibility.

Mandatory examples to resolve include deprecated symbol aliases, legacy public API env keys, old package/subpath doors, old browser-storage cleanup paths, old-shell notification fallback behavior and comments/tests named as compatibility boundaries.

Do not delete behavior blindly: characterize it first, preserve required observable behavior through the final owner, migrate durable state where necessary, then delete the compatibility mechanism itself.

**Required evidence:** a Compatibility Eradication Ledger with zero unresolved internal compatibility rows and a repository scan proving no deprecated/legacy architectural alias remains reachable.
---

## 30. `src/core/api` and `src/core/config` Decomposition

**Objective:** Remove two current non-package ownership hubs that would otherwise survive the package refactor and continue coupling unrelated runtimes/capabilities.

### API boundary

- `http-transport-core` becomes the sole low-level `fetch` execution gateway.
- `api-client-core` owns ASOL request/response protocol, URL construction, typed errors and transport mapping only.
- `api-read-policy-core` owns route freshness classification and cache-key semantics only.
- `query-cache-core` owns cache state/persistence; the API client consumes a cache port and does not own TanStack Query.
- telemetry is supplied through `telemetry-contract-core`; API transport does not import observability runtime implementation.
- notification-grant scheduling is supplied through an explicit response-effect port wired by composition; API parsing does not import notification bridge implementation.
- `service-routing-core` owns route ownership; `service-origin-config-core` supplies origins. Neither owns HTTP execution.

### Configuration boundary

- `env-core` owns pure environment-map value parsing only. It MUST require an explicitly supplied source and MUST NOT default to `process.env`; server/tooling/client build adapters supply their approved maps at composition/build boundaries.
- `runtime-context-core` owns pure runtime-context resolution only.
- service origins, Turso runtime/platform credentials, sessions, password recovery, notification grant/FCM/APNs/Web Push/mobile-push client and server credentials, storage, OTA, release callback, mail, app links, public app metadata and system-log configuration each use their dedicated configuration owner from Section 8.
- remove the broad `server-env`/`public-env` knowledge surface after all consumers move; no replacement mega-config package is allowed.
- configuration packages may expose typed values but never execute use cases or construct privileged clients.

**Required evidence:** `src/core/api` and broad capability-owning `src/core/config` code reach zero canonical responsibilities in the ledger; remaining host files are selectors/adapters only.
---

## 31. Tooling, Root Scripts and Executable Ownership

**Objective:** Prevent `scripts/` and root npm commands from becoming an unsealed parallel architecture after runtime packages are cleaned up.

Inventory every root `package.json` script and every production-maintenance script. For each command, record the capability it invokes, final tooling owner, inputs, side effects and verification gate.

Rules:

1. reusable release/build/migration/provisioning/catalog/native logic lives in its tooling owner package;
2. root scripts are thin command selectors/orchestrators only;
3. root scripts MUST NOT import superseded package internals;
4. domain-specific migration logic belongs to the domain's data/tooling owner, not a generic migration dumping ground;
5. a tooling package keeps one import door (`.`); if an executable `bin` entry is required, it is registered as that same package's canonical executable surface and MUST NOT expose a second import API;
6. generated service/build artifacts are outputs, never implementation owners;
7. script tests migrate with the capability they characterize rather than remaining attached to obsolete paths.

Explicitly migrate current root-script references into `data-core/src/tooling`, `ota-core` tooling, `native-core` validation/build scripts, release internals and any other removed package internals. `scripts/provision-mobile-push-credentials.ts` and `scripts/encrypt-mobile-push-credential-blob.ts` move into `mobile-push-provisioning-core`; their shared envelope algorithm moves to `mobile-push-crypto-core`, and the provisioning command MUST redact the raw unlock key from logs/output.

**Required evidence:** root script inventory has zero old/internal-owner references, each command resolves to one canonical tooling owner, and all script-level behavior tests pass.
---

## 32. Exact Runtime/Composition Topology

**Objective:** Satisfy the runtime-specific composition requirement literally for all five application surfaces and all seven isolated services, rather than sharing a broad root whose branches are decided after import.

| Runtime/deployment | Final composition package | Composition objective |
| --- | --- | --- |
| local Development | `@asol/gova-development-composition` | wire the complete local browser/server route graph using dev-only configuration/adapters; request-runtime composition imports no repository/release tooling modules, while child commands remain tooling boundaries |
| Web production | `@asol/gova-web-composition` | wire the production web/frontend graph and only reachable web runtime capabilities |
| Static export | `@asol/gova-static-composition` | build/export without server-only drivers, secrets or Business API execution packages |
| Android | `@asol/gova-android-composition` | wire Android-reachable browser/runtime/native capabilities and exclude iOS/server-only capability owners |
| iOS | `@asol/gova-ios-composition` | wire iOS-reachable browser/runtime/native capabilities and exclude Android/server-only capability owners |
| Web Push Service Worker | `@asol/web-push-worker-composition` | wire only push event/display/click behavior, notification-center persistence and specialty-receipt mutation; emit the owned `public/asol-push-sw.js` artifact |
| control | `@asol/control-composition` | wire only operational control routes/use cases/data/config with control credentials |

Control request runtime explicitly includes the **application/data/infrastructure** owners for Build Jobs, Artifact Analysis, Google Play operations and remote release control because live Control routes execute those use cases. It explicitly excludes `release-pipeline-core`, `release-environment-loader-core`, `env-file-core`, `service-mirror-core` and `architecture-core`: release/build commands invoke those tooling capabilities only in approved child/CLI/sandbox execution contexts. This distinction is load-bearing for least privilege and for keeping repository tooling out of the deployed request module graph.
| notifications | `@asol/notifications-composition` | wire notification grant/delivery/provider/data capabilities only |
| orders | `@asol/orders-composition` | wire order read/use-case/data capabilities only |
| products | `@asol/products-composition` | wire product/search/catalog read capabilities and only required image-read adapters |
| profiles | `@asol/profiles-composition` | wire profile/discount/image-read capabilities only |
| submain | `@asol/submain-composition` | wire its final route ledger's independent owners without becoming a business owner itself |
| sub2main | `@asol/sub2main-composition` | wire its final write/review/storage owners without becoming a business owner itself |

`src/instrumentation.ts`, static build entrypoints and native bootstraps select the relevant root; they do not assemble capabilities themselves.

The Section 36 dependency allowlist currently yields the following **target direct/closure budgets**. These are upper bounds, not quotas: implementation may reduce a root by removing unused edges, but adding an edge requires an intentional plan/registry change with reachability evidence.

| Composition | Target | Direct package allowlist | Transitive closure |
| --- | --- | ---: | ---: |
| `@asol/gova-development-composition` | Development | 152 | 194 |
| `@asol/gova-web-composition` | Web | 51 | 70 |
| `@asol/gova-static-composition` | Static | 51 | 70 |
| `@asol/gova-android-composition` | Android | 83 | 105 |
| `@asol/gova-ios-composition` | iOS | 83 | 105 |
| `@asol/web-push-worker-composition` | Web Push Worker | 4 | 7 |
| `@asol/control-composition` | control | 49 | 70 |
| `@asol/notifications-composition` | notifications | 18 | 28 |
| `@asol/orders-composition` | orders | 10 | 18 |
| `@asol/products-composition` | products | 17 | 29 |
| `@asol/profiles-composition` | profiles | 12 | 20 |
| `@asol/submain-composition` | submain | 48 | 71 |
| `@asol/sub2main-composition` | sub2main | 24 | 39 |

Client-root negative budgets are binding: Web/Static/Android/iOS closures contain **zero server-only DB drivers, zero server-only secret configuration packages and zero tooling packages**. The notifications service closure contains **zero** user-database or Mobile Push unlock packages; `submain` is the sole service closure containing the server unlock family.

**Required evidence:** thirteen composition-registry rows above, exact transitive closures, positive/negative capability assertions, startup secret-set validation and measured before/after closure metrics.

## 33. Objective Requirement for Every Execution Action

**Objective:** Ensure no implementation action is performed merely because it appears mechanically necessary. Every edit/move/delete/create/wiring/gate action must state what architectural property it establishes and how success is proven.

Every implementation checklist item derived from this plan MUST contain these fields before execution:

- **Action ID** — stable identifier;
- **Action** — exact files/packages/contracts affected;
- **Objective** — why this action is necessary for the goal;
- **Ownership transition** — old owner → final owner, or `none` for enforcement/verification;
- **Layer effect** — layer boundary established or protected;
- **Runtime/deployment effect** — closures changed and why;
- **Compatibility removal** — old path/shim/alias removed, if any;
- **Feature-preservation evidence** — behavior/tests/contracts that prove no loss;
- **Verification** — exact static/behavior/runtime gate proving completion;
- **Completion condition** — observable condition that turns the action DONE.

An action without an explicit **Objective** and **Verification** is not executable under this plan.

The Objective statements in Steps 1–12 define the parent objective for their child actions. Each child action must additionally state its specific objective in the implementation ledger, not merely inherit a generic “refactor package” purpose.

**Required evidence:** final Action Ledger has zero rows missing `Objective`, `Ownership transition`, `Feature-preservation evidence`, `Verification` or `Completion condition`.
---

## 34. `native-core` Exhaustive Source-to-Owner Rule

**Objective:** Guarantee that deleting `native-core` does not lose or silently regroup any of its current platform capabilities.

The current TypeScript adapters/APIs for action sheet, app/back-button, background download, barcode, browser, camera, clipboard, device, dialog, files, haptics, HTTP, keyboard, location, network, notifications, permissions, preferences, screen orientation, share, speech, splash screen, status bar, storage capacity, text zoom and toast map one-for-one to the corresponding packages in Section 8.9. The audit also covers the package's Android Java and iOS Swift sources, not only `src/`.

Additional mapping rules:

- shared result/error/listener/emitter/lazy-plugin/platform-global mechanics → `native-platform-core` only;
- capability key/shell capability registry → `native-platform-core` if runtime-facing, and its generated plugin/build projection → `native-build-core`;
- `adapters/ota` and `api/ota` do NOT create a second OTA owner: their OTA-specific orchestration moves to `ota-runtime-core`, which consumes `native-app-core`, `native-files-core` and other platform ports rather than importing their plugin SDKs directly;
- each validation schema moves with the capability whose external input it validates;
- Android notification channels/inbox/push service and iOS Firebase token bridging → `native-notifications-core`; `AppDelegate.swift` must no longer directly own Firebase Messaging behavior after cutover;
- Android/iOS background-download native plugins → `native-download-core`;
- Android/iOS native crash plugins/stores/reporters → `native-crash-core`;
- Android `ShareReceivePlugin` and iOS `ShareExtension` inbox/manifest bridge → `native-share-receive-core`;
- Android/iOS storage-capacity plugins → `native-storage-capacity-core`;
- app-settings native plugins → `native-preferences-core`;
- `MainActivity.java` and `AppDelegate.swift` become thin native-host composition artifacts owned by the Android/iOS composition roots and contain no provider/business policy;
- native tests move with the owner they characterize; cross-capability host behavior becomes composition/integration tests rather than a replacement native mega-package.

`permission-core` owns the platform-neutral permission vocabulary. `web-permissions-core` owns browser permission APIs. `native-permissions-core` may orchestrate native permission requests across capability ports, but it may not import camera/geolocation/notification/speech plugin SDKs directly. Those SDKs remain with their capability owners. Browser fallbacks currently embedded in `native-core` adapters MUST move to the matching `web-*` package; final native packages contain no browser implementation branches.

**Required evidence:** every production file currently under `packages/native-core/src` has exactly one final-owner ledger row, every plugin dependency has one privileged adapter owner, and the `native-core` folder is deleted with zero forwarding surface.
---

## 35. Notifications Mega-Feature Decomposition Rule

**Objective:** Prevent the current large `src/features/notifications` implementation from becoming a second notification mega-package after `notifications-core` is narrowed.

Map the current feature by responsibility, not folder name:

- canonical notification entities/templates/enums/mapping/validation policy → `notifications-core`;
- grant issue/read/validate protocol → `notification-grants-core`;
- server fan-out/send orchestration → `notification-delivery-core`;
- server notification token/push-preference/recipient persistence → `notification-data-core`;
- specialty-chat preference persistence currently misplaced under notification data → `specialty-chat-data-core`;
- device-local notification list/dismissed/badge state → `notification-center-data-core`;
- device-local notification settings → `notification-settings-data-core`;
- device-local push token/id/opt-in state → `notification-device-local-data-core`;
- offline replay queue → `notification-retry-data-core`;
- lifecycle analytics records → `notification-analytics-data-core`;
- center list/read/dismiss/open/extensions → `notification-center-core`;
- device registration/permission/token refresh lifecycle → `notification-device-core`;
- retry/reconciliation/synchronization lifecycle → `notification-sync-core`;
- native plugin calls/channels/tray operations → `native-notifications-core`;
- server Web Push/FCM/APNs provider transport → their infrastructure owners; device-direct FCM transport → `fcm-device-core`; browser PushManager/service-worker subscription → `web-push-client-core`; browser Notification API/badges → `web-notifications-core`;
- HTTP calls → `api-client-core` from application owners, never a notification-owned fetch adapter;
- React notification center/settings/controllers → `notification-ui-core` or host-only page presentation where not reusable.

The current broad notification facade MUST NOT survive as a package that re-owns all these concerns. If the application wants one convenience object, it may be a host-only composition facade with no canonical types, policy, persistence, provider code or privileged imports, and it must not be required by service compositions.

**Required evidence:** every non-test production file in the current notifications feature has one ledger owner, no final notification package spans multiple layers, and client/server/native notification runtime tests preserve current behavior.

---

**Required evidence:** `architecture-core` ingests an equivalent machine-readable registry, computes the live graph and authority maps, and fails on any package, door, runtime target, vendor owner, I/O permission, secret access, direct edge or composition membership that differs from this manifest without an intentional protected architecture-contract change. The generated target graph MUST prove zero unknown package reference, zero cycle, zero forbidden layer edge, zero composition-to-composition edge and zero runtime-target mismatch.

## 37. Exhaustive Execution Action Ledger

**Objective:** Make the phrase “every action has a purpose” literal. Nothing in the implementation may be executed as an unreasoned mechanical move. Each action below states the architectural property it creates, the ownership transition, runtime/layer effect, compatibility removal, preservation evidence, verification and objective completion condition.

The ledger is binding together with Sections 15, 33 and 36. If implementation discovery requires a new action, add a row with all fields **before** executing it. A command/edit/move/delete that changes architecture but has no Action ID is forbidden.

### 37.1 Cross-cutting actions

| Action ID | Action | Objective | Ownership transition | Layer/runtime effect | Compatibility removal | Feature-preservation evidence | Verification | Completion condition |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| INV-001 | Freeze repository baseline and preserve pre-existing local changes | Create a reproducible starting point and prevent unrelated work from being overwritten during the atomic refactor. | none → baseline evidence | all surfaces observed; no architecture change | none | record HEAD/status and protected dirty paths | `git status --short`; baseline report | baseline identifies HEAD, dirty paths and protected files |
| INV-002 | Inventory all 48 current sealed packages | Ensure every existing package receives KEEP/NARROW/REWRITE/REMOVE ownership treatment. | current packages → responsibility ledger | all layers | none | compare registry and filesystem | registry/filesystem diff script | 48/48 packages classified with zero extras/missing |
| INV-003 | Inventory all 45 registered application features | Prevent canonical capability ownership from remaining hidden in `src/features`. | feature-owned capability rows → final owners | application host boundary | none | feature tests/source map | feature-registry coverage script | 45/45 features classified |
| INV-004 | Inventory non-package ownership surfaces | Cover `src/core`, `src/shared`, `src/app`, scripts, root config, Android, iOS and public artifacts. | unsealed ownership → package or registered external artifact | all runtimes/native/tooling | none | path/source ownership map | unowned-path scanner | zero production/tooling path remains UNOWNED |

### 37.2 Per-package materialization actions

Every final package has its own executable action. The package row is generated conceptually from Section 36: if responsibility/layer/runtime/dependencies change, this action must be updated in the same plan change. A family-level migration can never hide a package that was not actually created, narrowed, tested, wired and cleared of its old owner.

### 37.3 Action-ledger completion invariant

- every Section 36 package has exactly one `PKG-*` row;
- every composition has a dedicated `COM-*` row in addition to its package-materialization row;
- every route/deployment/secret reassignment has a `ROUTE-*` row;
- every old-owner deletion and compatibility cleanup has a `CUT-*` row;
- verification/documentation cannot be marked complete until all migration/cutover rows are DONE;
- the final implementation report lists PASS/FAIL evidence for every action ID; no “implicitly completed” action exists.

**Required evidence:** action-ledger validator reports full Section 36 package coverage, unique Action IDs, no blank Objective/Ownership/Verification/Completion fields, and every row reaches DONE before the refactor is declared complete.


---

## 38. Goal-to-Plan Traceability Matrix

**Objective:** Prove that executing this plan is sufficient to satisfy every requirement in `not/package-architecture-refactor-goal.md`. A goal is not considered covered merely because a related package name exists; it must have an implementation mechanism and a blocking acceptance proof.

| Goal requirement | Plan mechanism | Objective of the mechanism | Blocking proof before completion |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Inspect every current package by actual responsibility | Sections 2, 7, 14; `INV-002`, Responsibility Ledger | prevent package names from hiding mixed ownership | live registry/filesystem = 48/48; every production/tooling file classified; zero unclassified responsibility |
| Split every package with independent responsibilities | Sections 7–9, 25, 34–35; `MIG-*`, `PKG-*` | replace mixed owners with responsibility-aligned package boundaries without micro-package theater | every final package passes Admission Test and Section 20; every REMOVE/NARROW source ledger closes |
| Create packages for ownerless boundaries | Sections 8, 13, 27, 36 | give every canonical capability/runtime/config/transport/platform concern a proper sealed owner | 316/316 manifest/package-action parity; zero UNOWNED production/tooling path |
| Exactly one owner per responsibility | Sections 5, 16, 20–21; `ENF-005`, `VER-011` | make ownership uniqueness machine-enforced | duplicate-owner/entity/repository scanners report zero conflicts |
| Eliminate dual ownership | Section 16 and Cutover actions | prevent old and new implementations from remaining reachable together | every migration row reaches `OLD_OWNER_DELETED`; zero forwarding/fallback owner remains |
| Package-level SRP | Sections 3, 20, 25, 36 | ensure one independent reason to change per package while preventing over-fragmentation | 316/316 Admission PASS; acceptance question 1–12 all YES per package |
| Explicit Domain/Application/Infrastructure/Data/Runtime-Config/Transport/Platform/Composition separation | Sections 3, 5, 36 | encode layer meaning and dependency direction instead of relying on conventions | zero forbidden layer edge; exactly one layer per package; registry vocabulary equals plan |
| Minimize implementation knowledge and dependency graph | Sections 3, 18, 36; `ENF-003/004`, `VER-007` | make every direct edge default-deny and every runtime closure reachability-driven | 1036 authorized target edges form a DAG; implementation may reduce but may not add an edge without proof |
| Smaller/faster/precise Composition Root | Sections 11, 18, 32, 36; `COM-*` | remove register-all wiring and give each runnable context only its reachable closure | 13 exact roots; closure budgets and negative assertions pass; no unused registration/dynamic import without measured reason |
| Review/split/replace existing compositions | Sections 7, 11, 32; all `COM-*` | treat current compositions as evidence, not sacred boundaries | all 7 current compositions receive REWRITE decisions; 6 new runtime/worker roots cover missing contexts; old seam exceptions deleted |
| One Capability → One Owner → One Public Door → One Explicit Composition | Sections 4–5, 13, 16, 32, 36 | turn the governing principle into registry/checkable facts | one ownership row, one `.` export, no deep/subpath door, exact composition membership |
| Preserve all features | Sections 19.3–19.7, 21; `INV-010`, `CONS-006`, `VER-001/012–018` | require behavioral parity before old code can be deleted | characterization/API/persistence/native/Control parity and all build/smoke gates pass |
| No temporary shared ownership in final state | Sections 15–16, 23; `CUT-*` | permit sequencing in one working branch without permitting a dual-authority final tree | no old owner remains after its consumer migration; no published dual package/API |
| No compatibility layers after final cutover | Sections 16, 23, 29; `ENF-011`, `CUT-003/006/007`, `VER-009` | remove aliases, forwarding exports, old/new APIs and migration fallbacks after state migration | Compatibility Eradication Ledger has zero internal compatibility row; repository scan clean |
| Every new unit is a proper sealed Gova package | Sections 13, 20, 36–37 | prevent modules/folders from substituting for governed packages | package.json + registry + one export + tests/gate + runtime/vendor/dependency metadata for 316/316 packages |
| Exact allowed dependencies/public door for every final package | Sections 4 and 36 | satisfy the goal literally with a package-specific allowlist | Section 8 set equals Section 36 set exactly; every live edge is declared; every package exports only `.` |
| Explicit remain/remove/split/new decisions | Sections 7–8, 37.2 | make final topology deterministic, not an implementation-time guess | 48/48 current packages have disposition; 316/316 targets have materialization actions |
| Final runtime/deployment composition topology | Sections 11, 28, 32, 36 | align wiring, routes, data and secrets with actual execution contexts | Development/Web/Static/Android/iOS/Worker + 7 service roots all pass exact closure and secret-set checks |
| Entire repository, not only `data-core` | Sections 14, 26–27, 31, 34–35 | cover `src`, scripts, services, public artifacts and native code as ownership surfaces | 45/45 feature rows, non-package ownership scan, Android/Swift maps and root-script inventory contain zero ownerless capability |

If any row above lacks its blocking proof at implementation time, the goal file has **not** been achieved and the refactor MUST NOT be declared complete.

---

## 39. No-Break / No-Loss / No-Gap Cutover Barrier

**Objective:** Make preservation a precondition for deletion and completion so an architecturally clean graph cannot mask lost behavior, data, routes, platform support or operational capability.

The implementation MUST obey these barriers in order:

1. **Characterize before move.** A responsibility cannot enter `MOVED` state until its observable contract has a baseline test/snapshot or an explicit deterministic characterization action.
2. **Materialize before redirect.** The final package, its one-door API, dependencies, layer metadata and targeted tests must exist before any production consumer is redirected to it.
3. **Redirect all consumers atomically.** Application, services, scripts, tests, native hosts and generated-source roots must switch in the same refactor branch; do not publish or merge an intermediate dual-contract state.
4. **Verify before delete.** Old source may be deleted only after targeted behavior tests and architecture checks pass through the final owner.
5. **Migrate state before removing compatibility reads.** Durable SQL/SQLite/Turso/AsolDB/WebStorage state must be migrated or proven representation-identical before any legacy reader/key/schema compatibility path is deleted.
6. **Delete before completion.** A capability is not complete at `MOVED`; it is complete only at `OLD_OWNER_DELETED`, with import/full-text scans proving the old authority cannot be reached.
7. **Verify every execution surface.** Development, Web, isolated Static, Android, iOS, Web Push Worker and all seven service deployments must pass their applicable non-publishing verification.
8. **Verify every external boundary.** HTTP, database/store, environment/secret, public artifact, native bridge and provider boundaries must pass pre/post parity or an explicitly planned migration.
9. **Fail on unexplained drift.** A changed route contract, schema/index, persisted key, capability result, runtime closure, required credential, generated artifact or provider behavior requires an explicit plan amendment and evidence; it cannot be accepted as incidental refactor fallout.
10. **Rollback is source-control rollback, not runtime compatibility.** Recovery from a failed implementation uses the working branch/commit history and preserved baseline evidence. Do not keep compatibility packages, aliases, fallback APIs or dual owners as a rollback mechanism.
11. **No release side effects during verification.** Verification may compile/build/smoke, but must not publish Vercel production, Play production, TestFlight/App Store, OTA releases or mutate production data merely to prove architecture.
12. **Completion is fail-closed.** Any unresolved ledger row, failed parity check, failed build/runtime/service/native gate, missing target package, extra dependency edge, compatibility residue or ownerless source blocks completion.

### Required final evidence bundle

The final implementation report must contain or link to all of the following machine-readable evidence:

- baseline and final package/feature/source Responsibility Ledgers;
- Section 36 live-registry parity and package graph report;
- 211-row baseline/final HTTP contract comparison;
- persistence/schema/migration/shard/browser-store parity report;
- environment/secret ownership and least-privilege report;
- privileged vendor-owner report;
- external artifact-owner parity report;
- native TypeScript/Android Java/iOS Swift source-to-owner report;
- 13 composition direct/transitive closure and negative-capability report;
- behavior-test mapping from old responsibility to final owner;
- Web/isolated Static/Android/iOS/service/Control verification results;
- Compatibility Eradication Ledger;
- final action ledger with every row `DONE` and evidence attached.

The absence of any required evidence item is itself a completion failure.
